Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindromic time
3. A palindromic time reads the same forwards and backwards (e.g., 05:50 → 05:50 backwards is 05:50)

## Key Insights

1. A time hh:mm is palindromic if when we reverse the string "hh:mm", we get the same string
2. For hh:mm, the reverse would be "mm:hh" but we need to check if it reads the same
3. Actually, we need to check if the entire string "hh:mm" reversed equals the original string

For example:
- "05:39" reversed is "93:50" → not equal to "05:39"
- "05:50" reversed is "05:50" → equal, so it's a palindrome

## Approach

1. Parse the input to get hours and minutes
2. Check if the current time is already a palindrome
3. If yes, return 0
4. If no, iterate forward from current time, checking each subsequent minute until we find a palindromic time
5. Return the number of minutes needed

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Parse input**: Extract hours and minutes from "hh:mm"
2. **Check if current time is palindrome**: Convert to string and check if it equals its reverse
3. **If not palindromic**: Start from current time and increment by 1 minute each time, checking palindromic property
4. **Handle time wraparound**: When minutes reach 60, hours increase by 1 (and hours wrap at 24)

## Implementation

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Remove spaces and check if string reads same forwards and backwards
    return time_str == time_str[::-1]

def time_to_minutes(hh, mm):
    return hh * 60 + mm

def minutes_to_time(t):
    hours = t // 60
    minutes = t % 60
    # Normalize hours to 0-23
    hours = hours % 24
    return hours, minutes

def solve(current_time):
    # Parse input
    h, m = map(int, current_time.split(':'))
    
    # Check if current time is already palindrome
    current_str = f"{h:02d}:{m:02d}"
    if is_palindrome(current_str):
        return 0
    
    # Start from current time and check each subsequent minute
    current_minutes = time_to_minutes(h, m)
    
    # We need to find the next palindromic time
    while True:
        current_minutes += 1
        
        # Convert back to hours and minutes
        h_next, m_next = minutes_to_time(current_minutes)
        
        # Format as string with leading zeros
        next_str = f"{h_next:02d}:{m_next:02d}"
        
        # Check if this time is palindrome
        if is_palindrome(next_str):
            return current_minutes - time_to_minutes(h, m)

# Read input
current_time = input().strip()
print(solve(current_time))
```

Wait, I need to be more careful about the time calculation. Let me fix this:

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindromic time
3. A palindromic time reads the same forwards and backwards when interpreted as a string
4. For example, "05:50" is a palindrome because "05:50" reversed is "05:50"
5. Time wraps around (23:59 → 00:00)

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Understand palindrome checking
For a time "hh:mm", when reversed it becomes "mm:hh". For it to be a palindrome, "hh:mm" should equal "mm:hh" when reversed.

So we need: `hhmm` (as a string) to read the same forwards and backwards.

For time "hh:mm", the reversed string is "mm:hh". For it to be a palindrome:
- The first 2 digits (hh) should equal the last 2 digits (mm)
- The middle colon stays in place

Actually, let me think again. The time "05:50" reversed is "05:50" - this works because:
- Original: "05:50"
- Reversed: "05:50" (when we reverse the entire string)

So the condition is that the string "hh:mm" reversed should equal the original string.

This means: the first two characters should equal the last two characters.

For "05:50": 
- First two: "05"
- Last two: "50" 
- Wait, that doesn't match.

Let me trace "05:50" backwards:
- "05:50" backwards is "05:50" (character by character: 0,5,:,5,0 → reversed: 0,5,:,5,0)

Actually, when we reverse "05:50", we get "05:50" because:
- Original: positions 0:'0', 1:'5', 2:',', 3:'5', 4:'0'
- Reversed: positions 0:'0', 1:'5', 2:',', 3:'5', 4:'0'

So "05:50" is a palindrome because it reads the same forwards and backwards.

The condition is: the string "hh:mm" when reversed should equal itself.

### Step 2: Identify palindromic times
For a time hh:mm to be palindromic:
- The first character (h1) should equal the last character (m2)
- The second character (h2) should equal the second-to-last character (m1)
- The middle colon stays in place

So: h1 = m2 and h2 = m1

For "05:50": h1=0, h2=5, m1=5, m2=0 → 0=0 and 5=5 ✓

For "13:31": h1=1, h2=3, m1=3, m2=1 → 1≠1? Wait, 1=1 and 3=3 ✓

"13:31" reversed is "13:31" → yes, it's a palindrome.

### Step 3: Algorithm
1. Parse the input time to get hours and minutes
2. Check if current time is already palindromic
3. If yes, return 0
4. If not, find the next palindromic time (considering time wraparound)
5. Calculate the minutes difference

### Step 4: Find next palindromic time
We need to check each possible time from current time onwards, until we find a palindromic one.

Since we have 24 hours = 1440 minutes, we can safely iterate through all possible times.

For each time, check if it's palindromic:
- Convert hh:mm to string "hh:mm"
- Reverse the string and compare
- If equal, it's a palindrome

### Step 5: Implementation

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Convert to string and reverse
    return time_str == time_str[::-1]

def solve():
    time_str = input().strip()
    hours, minutes = map(int, time_str.split(':'))
    
    # Convert to total minutes since 00:00
    current_minutes = hours * 60 + minutes
    
    # Check if current time is palindromic
    if is_palindrome(f"{hours:02d}:{minutes:02d}"):
        return 0
    
    # Find the next palindromic time
    for next_minutes in range(current_minutes + 1, current_minutes + 1440):
        # Convert back to hours and minutes
        next_hours = (next_minutes // 60) % 24
        next_minutes_remaining = next_minutes % 60
        
        # Format the time as "hh:mm"
        time_str = f"{next_hours:02d}:{next_minutes_remaining:02d}"
        
        if is_palindrome(time_str):
            return (next_minutes - current_minutes) % 1440
    
    # This should never happen
    return 0

print(solve())
```

Example 1: "05:39"
- Current time: 05:39 → reversed is "93:50" ≠ "05:39" → not palindrome
- Next palindromic time: 05:50 (since 05:50 reversed is 05:50)
- Minutes from 05:39 to 05:50 = 11 minutes ✓

Example 2: "13:31"
- 13:31 reversed is "13:31" → palindrome → return 0 ✓

Example 3: "23:59"
- 23:59 reversed is "95:32" ≠ "23:59" → not palindrome
- Next palindromic time: 00:00 (since 00:00 reversed is "00:00")
- Minutes from 23:59 to 00:00 = 1 minute ✓

The solution looks correct!

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum minutes to sleep until the next palindromic time
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards (e.g., 05:50 → 05:50 reversed is 05:50)

## Key Insights

1. We need to check if the current time is already a palindrome
2. If not, we need to find the next palindromic time
3. Since we're dealing with 24-hour format, times wrap around from 23:59 to 00:00
4. A palindromic time has the property that when reversed, it reads the same

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Define what makes a time palindromic
For time "hh:mm", when reversed, it becomes "mm:hh". For it to be a palindrome:
- The string "hh:mm" reversed should equal "hh:mm"
- So we need: h1h2:m1m2 → m1m2:h2h1
- For it to be a palindrome: h1h2:m1m2 = m1m2:h2h1
- This means: h1 = m1, h2 = m2, and the time reads the same forwards and backwards

Actually, let me reconsider. For a time "hh:mm" to be a palindrome when read as a string:
- The string "hh:mm" reversed should equal "hh:mm"
- So "hh:mm" reversed = "mm:hh"
- We need "hh:mm" == "mm:hh"

So we need: h1 = m1 and h2 = m2.

### Step 2: Algorithm
1. Parse the input time
2. Check if current time is palindrome
3. If yes, return 0
4. If not, find the next palindromic time (either same day or next day)
5. Calculate minutes difference

### Step 3: Implementation details
- Current time: hh:mm
- We need to find the smallest time >= current time that forms a palindrome
- The palindromic condition: when we reverse "hh:mm" we get "mm:hh", and these should be equal
- So we need hh == mm in terms of digit matching

Wait, let me think again about palindromes. For "05:50":
- Original: "05:50"
- Reversed: "05:50" → reversed character by character: first char '0', then '5', then ':', then '5', then '0' → "05:50" reversed is "05:50" (same)

Actually, when we reverse "05:50", it becomes "05:50" because:
- Original: positions 0:'0', 1:'5', 2:',', 3:'5', 4:'0'
- Reversed: positions 4:'0', 3:'5', 2:',', 1:'5', 0:'0' → "05:50"

So the condition is that the string reads the same forwards and backwards.

For time hh:mm to be palindrome:
- Two digits of hours (h1,h2) and two digits of minutes (m1,m2)
- The string is: h1,h2,:,m1,m2
- Reversed: m2,m1,:,h2,h1
- For palindrome: h1,h2,:,m1,m2 = m2,m1,:,h2,h1
- This means: h1 = m2, h2 = m1, and the middle colon stays

So the condition is: h1 = m2 and h2 = m1.

### Step 4: Algorithm
1. Parse current time
2. Check if current time is palindrome
3. If not, find next palindromic time by iterating forward
4. For each time, check if it's palindrome using the condition above

Let me verify with examples:
- 05:39: is it palindrome? h1=0,h2=5,m1=3,m2=9 → 0=9? No → not palindrome
- Check 05:50: h1=0,h2=5,m1=5,m2=0 → 0=0 and 5=5 → yes, palindrome
- Minutes from 05:39 to 05:50 = 11 → matches expected

- 13:31: h1=1,h2=3,m1=3,m2=1 → 1=1 and 3=3 → palindrome → output 0 ✓

- 23:59: h1=2,h2=3,m1=5,m2=9 → 2=9? No → not palindrome
- Next palindromic time: 00:00 → h1=0,h2=0,m1=0,m2=0 → 0=0 and 0=0 → palindrome
- Minutes from 23:59 to 00:00 = (24*60 - 59) = 1440 - 59 = 1381... wait, that's not right.

Wait, from 23:59 to 00:00 is 1 minute? Let me think:
- 23:59 + 1 minute = 00:00 → that's 1 minute ✓

So the palindromic time 00:00 satisfies h1=0,h2=0,m1=0,m2=0 → 0=0 and 0=0.

So algorithm:
1. Parse input
2. Check if current time is palindrome
3. If yes, return 0
4. Otherwise, start from current time and check each subsequent time until we find a palindrome, returning the minutes difference

```python
def is_palindrome_time(hh, mm):
    # Convert to strings
    h1, h2 = str(hh)[0], str(hh)[1]
    m1, m2 = str(mm)[0], str(mm)[1]
    # For "hh:mm" to be palindrome when reversed, we need:
    # The string "hh:mm" reversed should equal "hh:mm"
    # Reversed: m2, m1, :, h2, h1
    # Original: h1, h2, :, m1, m2
    # So we need: h1 == m2 and h2 == m1
    return h1 == m2 and h2 == m1

def time_to_minutes(hh, mm):
    return hh * 60 + mm

def minutes_to_time(minutes):
    total = minutes % (24 * 60)
    hh = total // 60
    mm = total % 60
    return hh, mm

def solve():
    time_str = input().strip()
    hh, mm = map(int, time_str.split(':'))
    
    # Check if current time is palindrome
    if is_palindrome_time(hh, mm):
        print(0)
        return
    
    # Find next palindromic time
    current_minutes = time_to_minutes(hh, mm)
    total_minutes = 24 * 60
    
    # Check times in order, starting from current time
    for minutes in range(current_minutes, total_minutes):
        hh_new, mm_new = minutes_to_time(minutes)
        if is_palindrome_time(hh_new, mm_new):
            print(minutes - current_minutes)
            return

solve()
```

You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Karen is getting ready for a new school day!

 [Image] 

It is currently hh:mm, given in a 24-hour format. As you know, Karen loves palindromes, and she believes that it is good luck to wake up when the time is a palindrome.

What is the minimum number of minutes she should sleep, such that, when she wakes up, the time is a palindrome?

Remember that a palindrome is a string that reads the same forwards and backwards. For instance, 05:39 is not a palindrome, because 05:39 backwards is 93:50. On the other hand, 05:50 is a palindrome, because 05:50 backwards is 05:50.


-----Input-----

The first and only line of input contains a single string in the format hh:mm (00 ≤  hh  ≤ 23, 00 ≤  mm  ≤ 59).


-----Output-----

Output a single integer on a line by itself, the minimum number of minutes she should sleep, such that, when she wakes up, the time is a palindrome.


-----Examples-----
Input
05:39

Output
11

Input
13:31

Output
0

Input
23:59

Output
1



-----Note-----

In the first test case, the minimum number of minutes Karen should sleep for is 11. She can wake up at 05:50, when the time is a palindrome.

In the second test case, Karen can wake up immediately, as the current time, 13:31, is already a palindrome.

In the third test case, the minimum number of minutes Karen should sleep for is 1 minute. She can wake up at 00:00, when the time is a palindrome.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes until the next palindromic time
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards (e.g., 05:50 → 05:50 backwards is 05:50)

## Key Insights

1. A time hh:mm is a palindrome if the string "hh:mm" reads the same forwards and backwards
2. For hh:mm to be a palindrome: 
   - First digit of hh must equal last digit of hh
   - First digit of mm must equal second digit of mm (but we have to consider the string format)
3. The full string is "hhmm" - to be palindrome, we need:
   - h1 == h2 (first digit of hh = second digit of hh)
   - h2 == m1 (second digit of hh = first digit of mm)
   - m1 == m2 (first digit of mm = second digit of mm)

Wait, let me reconsider. The time is in format "hh:mm". When reversed, it becomes "mm:hh" reversed. Actually, let me think about this more carefully.

Actually, when we reverse "hh:mm", we get "m2m1:h2h1" (where h2h1 is the original hh reversed, and m2m1 is the original mm reversed).

For "hh:mm" to be a palindrome when reversed, we need:
- The original string "hh:mm" reversed should equal the original string
- This means: h1h2:m1m2 reversed = m2m1:h2h1 should equal h1h2:m1m2

So for the string to be palindrome: h1h2:m1m2 = m2m1:h2h1

This gives us constraints:
- h1 = m2 (first digit of hh = second digit of mm)
- h2 = m1 (second digit of hh = first digit of mm)
- m1 = h2 (first digit of mm = second digit of hh) 
- m2 = h1 (second digit of mm = first digit of hh)

So the constraints are: h1 = m2 and h2 = m1.

Therefore, for time hh:mm to be palindrome: 
- First digit of hh must equal second digit of mm
- Second digit of hh must equal first digit of mm

## Solution Approach

1. Parse the input time to get hh and mm
2. Check if current time is already palindrome
3. If not, find the next palindromic time by incrementing minutes until we find one
4. Return the difference in minutes

Since we're dealing with hours and minutes, we need to handle the 24-hour cycle properly.

## Step-by-step Algorithm

1. Parse input to get hours and minutes
2. Create a function to check if a time is palindrome
3. Starting from current time, increment by 1 minute at a time, check if palindrome
4. Stop when we find the first palindromic time
5. Return the difference in minutes

But we also need to handle the case where the next palindrome might be the next day (like 23:59 → 00:00).

Let me check the example: 23:59 → 00:00
- 23:59 reversed is 95:32 ≠ 23:59
- Need to find next palindrome
- After 23:59 comes 00:00
- Is 00:00 a palindrome? 00:00 reversed is 00:00 → yes!
- Difference: from 23:59 to 00:00 is 1 minute

For 05:39 → 05:50
- 05:39 reversed is 93:50 ≠ 05:39
- 05:40 → 40:50 → no
- ... until 05:50 → 05:50 reversed is 05:50 → yes!
- Difference: 11 minutes

## Implementation

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # time_str is in format "hh:mm"
    # Remove colon and check if reversed string equals original
    s = time_str.replace(':', '')
    return s == s[::-1]

def time_to_minutes(h, m):
    return h * 60 + m

def minutes_to_time(total_minutes):
    h = (total_minutes // 60) % 24
    m = total_minutes % 60
    return h, m

# Read input
time_str = input().strip()
h, m = map(int, time_str.split(':'))

# Current time in minutes from 00:00
current_minutes = time_to_minutes(h, m)

# Find next palindromic time
total_minutes = current_minutes
while True:
    h, m = minutes_to_time(total_minutes)
    time_str = f"{h:02d}:{m:02d}"
    if is_palindrome(time_str):
        break
    total_minutes += 1

# Answer is difference in minutes
print(total_minutes - current_minutes)
```

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the time becomes a palindrome
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards (e.g., 05:50 → 05:50 backwards is 05:50)

## Key Insights

1. A time hh:mm is a palindrome if the string "hhmm" reads the same forwards and backwards
2. For time "hh:mm", when reversed it becomes "m'mh" where m' is the reversed minutes
3. For it to be a palindrome: hh:mm = mm:hh (when properly formatted)

## Analysis of Palindrome Condition

For time "hh:mm" to be a palindrome:
- The first digit of hh should equal the last digit of mm 
- The second digit of hh should equal the first digit of mm

Actually, let's think about it more carefully:
- Original time: "hh:mm" 
- Reverse: "mm:hh" (but we need to check if "hh:mm" == reverse of "hh:mm")

Actually, let me reconsider: "05:50" reversed is "05:50" → this works
"13:31" reversed is "13:31" → this works
"05:39" reversed is "93:50" → this doesn't work

For a time "hh:mm" to be a palindrome when reversed, we need:
- The string "hh:mm" reversed should equal "hh:mm"

So if we have "05:50", reversed is "05:50" → palindrome ✓
If we have "13:31", reversed is "13:31" → palindrome ✓
If we have "05:39", reversed is "93:50" → not equal → not palindrome

Wait, let me trace this:
- "05:39" → reversed is "93:50" → not equal to "05:39" → not palindrome
- "05:50" → reversed is "05:50" → equal → palindrome

So the palindrome condition: when we reverse the string "hh:mm", we should get back "hh:mm".

## Algorithm

1. Parse the input time into hours and minutes
2. Start from the current time and check each subsequent minute until we find a palindromic time
3. For each time, check if it's a palindrome by comparing the string with its reverse
4. Return the number of minutes until the first palindromic time

## Edge Case

When the current time is already a palindrome, return 0.

## Implementation

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Reverse the string and compare
    reversed_str = time_str[::-1]
    return time_str == reversed_str

def solve():
    time_str = input().strip()
    hours, minutes = map(int, time_str.split(':'))
    
    # Check if current time is already a palindrome
    if is_palindrome(time_str):
        return 0
    
    # Start checking from next minute
    current_minutes = minutes
    current_hours = hours
    
    # We need to check until we find a palindrome
    # Maximum we might need to go is 24*60 minutes (one full day)
    # But realistically, palindromic times are common enough that we won't need more than a few hours
    
    while True:
        # Convert current time to minutes since start of day
        total_minutes = current_hours * 60 + current_minutes
        
        # Next time (in minutes)
        next_total_minutes = total_minutes + 1
        
        # Convert back to hours and minutes
        next_hours = next_total_minutes // 60
        next_minutes = next_total_minutes % 60
        
        # Format the time string
        next_time_str = f"{next_hours:02d}:{next_minutes:02d}"
        
        # Check if this time is a palindrome
        if is_palindrome(next_time_str):
            return 1
        
        # Move to next minute
        current_minutes = next_minutes
        current_hours = next_hours
        
        # If we've gone through a full day, we should have found a palindrome
        # (Since palindromic times exist, like 00:00, 11:11, 20:02, etc.)
        if current_hours > 23:
            current_hours = 0
            current_minutes = 0
            # We've completed a full day - we should have found a palindrome
            # Actually, we need to break out of this loop and return a value
            pass
    
    # Actually, let's reconsider - the above loop might be problematic
    
    # Better approach: just check each minute incrementally until we find a palindrome
    current_minutes = minutes
    current_hours = hours
    
    # We know we need to find the minimum minutes to sleep
    # Let's just simulate minute by minute
    time_count = 1
    
    while True:
        # Check if current time is palindromic
        # We're checking the next time point
        next_hours = current_hours
        next_minutes = current_minutes
        
        # If we've gone past 23:59, wrap to 00:00
        if next_minutes == 60:
            next_minutes = 0
            next_hours += 1
            if next_hours > 23:
                next_hours = 0
        
        # Format the time string
        next_time_str = f"{next_hours:02d}:{next_minutes:02d}"
        
        # Check if palindromic
        if is_palindrome(next_time_str):
            return time_count
        
        # Move to next minute
        time_count += 1
        current_minutes += 1
        if current_minutes >= 60:
            current_minutes = 0
            current_hours += 1
            if current_hours > 23:
                current_hours = 0

# Actually, let me simplify and fix the logic
def solve():
    time_str = input().strip()
    hours, minutes = map(int, time_str.split(':'))
    
    # Check if current time is already palindrome
    current_time_str = f"{hours:02d}:{minutes:02d}"
    if is_palindrome(current_time_str):
        return 0
    
    # Check each minute from now onwards
    minutes_to_sleep = 1
    
    while True:
        # Calculate next time
        new_minutes = minutes + minutes_to_sleep
        new_hours = hours
        
        # Handle time wrapping
        while new_minutes >= 60:
            new_minutes -= 60
            new_hours += 1
            if new_hours > 23:
                new_hours = 0
        
        # Format the new time string
        new_time_str = f"{new_hours:02d}:{new_minutes:02d}"
        
        # Check if it's a palindrome
        if is_palindrome(new_time_str):
            return minutes_to_sleep
        
        minutes_to_sleep += 1
```

- Try +1: 05:40 → "05:40" reversed = "04:50" → not equal
- Try +2: 05:41 → "05:41" reversed = "14:50" → not equal

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    return time_str == time_str[::-1]

time_str = input().strip()
hours, minutes = map(int, time_str.split(':'))

# Check if current time is already a palindrome
current_time_str = f"{hours:02d}:{minutes:02d}"
if is_palindrome(current_time_str):
    print(0)
else:
    # Try each minute incrementally
    minutes_to_sleep = 1
    while True:
        # Calculate next time considering time wraparound
        new_minutes = minutes + minutes_to_sleep
        new_hours = hours
        
        # Add minutes to hours when necessary
        while new_minutes >= 60:
            new_minutes -= 60
            new_hours += 1
            if new_hours > 23:
                new_hours = 0
        
        # Format the new time string
        new_time_str = f"{new_hours:02d}:{new_minutes:02d}"
        
        # Check if it's a palindrome
        if is_palindrome(new_time_str):
            print(minutes_to_sleep)
            break
        
        minutes_to_sleep += 1
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindrome time
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards (e.g., 05:50 → 05:50 reversed is 05:50)

## Key Observations

1. A time like "05:50" is a palindrome because when reversed, it becomes "05:50"
2. The time format is hh:mm (4 digits: h1 h2 : m1 m2)
3. For a time to be a palindrome: h1 h2 : m1 m2 should equal m2 m1 : h2 h1 when reversed
4. So we need: h1 h2 : m1 m2 = m2 m1 : h2 h1 (when properly interpreted)

## Step-by-Step Analysis

1. The palindrome condition: 
   - The first digit (h1) should equal the last digit (m2)
   - The second digit (h2) should equal the second-to-last digit (m1)
   
2. So if we have time "ab:cd", it's a palindrome when:
   - a = d and b = c
   
3. We need to check times sequentially from the current time onwards (wrapping around if needed)

## Algorithm

1. Parse the input time into hours and minutes
2. Check if current time is already a palindrome
3. If yes, return 0
4. If not, iterate through future times (adding minutes) until we find a palindrome
5. The next palindrome could wrap from 23:59 to 00:00

## Implementation Details

- Convert time to total minutes since 00:00
- For each possible palindrome time, check if it's valid
- Start from current time and go forward
- For a time to be a palindrome, when we reverse the 4-character string, it should equal the original

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Remove colon and check if string reads same forwards and backwards
    digits = time_str.replace(':', '')
    return digits == digits[::-1]

def solve():
    time_str = input().strip()
    h, m = map(int, time_str.split(':'))
    
    # Convert to total minutes since 00:00
    current_minutes = h * 60 + m
    
    # Check if current time is already a palindrome
    if is_palindrome(time_str):
        return 0
    
    # Find next palindrome time
    total_minutes = 24 * 60  # 1440 minutes in a day
    
    for minutes in range(1, total_minutes):
        next_time_minutes = (current_minutes + minutes) % total_minutes
        
        # Convert back to hours and minutes
        next_h = (next_time_minutes // 60) % 24
        next_m = next_time_minutes % 60
        
        # Format as string for palindrome check
        next_time_str = f"{next_h:02d}:{next_m:02d}"
        
        if is_palindrome(next_time_str):
            return minutes
    
    return 0  # Should never reach here

print(solve())
```

Wait, let me trace through the examples to verify:

Example 1: "05:39"
- Current time: 05:39 → "0539" → reversed "9350" ≠ "0539" → not palindrome
- Next check: 05:40 → "0540" → "0450" ≠ "0540"
- Continue until 05:50 → "0550" → "0550" → palindrome!
- Minutes from 05:39 to 05:50 = 11 minutes ✓

Example 2: "13:31"
- "1331" → reversed "1331" → palindrome → 0 minutes ✓

Example 3: "23:59"
- "2359" → reversed "9532" ≠ "2359" → not palindrome
- Next: 00:00 → "0000" → reversed "0000" → palindrome
- Minutes from 23:59 to 00:00 = 1 minute ✓

The solution looks correct.

Karen is getting ready for a new school day!

 [Image]

What is the minimum number of minutes she should sleep, such that, when she wakes up, the time is a palindrome?

Remember that a palindrome is a string that reads the same forwards and backwards. For instance, 05:39 is not a palindrome, because 05:39 backwards is 93:50. On the other hand, 05:50 is a palindrome, because 05:50 backwards is 05:50.


-----Input-----

The first and only line of input contains a single string in the format hh:mm (00 ≤  hh  ≤ 23, 00 ≤  mm  ≤ 59).


-----Output-----

Output a single integer on a line by itself, the minimum number of minutes she should sleep, such that, when she wakes up, the time is a palindrome.


-----Examples-----
Input
05:39

Output
11

Input
13:31

Output
0

Input
23:59

Output
1



-----Note-----

In the first test case, the minimum number of minutes Karen should sleep for is 11. She can wake up at 05:50, when the time is a palindrome.

In the second test case, Karen can wake up immediately, as the current time, 13:31, is already a palindrome.

In the third test case, the minimum number of minutes Karen should sleep for is 1 minute. She can wake up at 00:00, when the time is a palindrome.

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindrome time
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards (e.g., 05:50 → 05:50 backwards is 05:50)

## Key Insights

1. A time is a palindrome if when we reverse the entire string, it reads the same
2. For a time "hh:mm", reversing it should give us the same string
3. For "hh:mm" to be a palindrome, the first digit of hh should equal the last digit of hh (reverse of first digit)
4. The first digit of mm should equal the second digit of mm (reverse of second digit)

## Step-by-Step Analysis

A time hh:mm is a palindrome if:
- The first digit of hh equals the last digit of hh (the units digit of hh)
- The first digit of mm equals the last digit of mm (the units digit of mm)

For example:
- 05:50 → 05:50 reversed is 05:50 (palindrome)
- 13:31 → 13:31 reversed is 13:31 (palindrome)
- 05:39 → 05:39 reversed is 93:50 (not palindrome)

## Algorithm

1. Parse the input time to get hours and minutes
2. Check if current time is already a palindrome
3. If yes, return 0
4. If no, find the next palindrome time by:
   - Starting from the current time
   - Incrementing minutes one by one
   - After 60 minutes, wrap to next hour
   - After 24 hours, wrap to 00:00
   - Check if each time is a palindrome
   - Return the first palindrome time found

## Implementation Strategy

1. Create a function to check if a time is a palindrome
2. Start from current time and keep adding minutes until we find a palindrome
3. Handle time wraparound (24-hour clock)

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # time_str is in format "hh:mm"
    # Convert to string and check if it reads the same forwards and backwards
    # We need to consider that when reversed, the time should be the same
    # For "hh:mm" reversed should be "mm:hh" but the actual reverse of string is "mm:hh" backwards
    # Actually, we need to reverse the entire string and compare
    reversed_str = time_str[::-1]
    return time_str == reversed_str

def solve(current_time):
    h, m = map(int, current_time.split(':'))
    
    # Check if current time is already palindrome
    if is_palindrome(current_time):
        return 0
    
    # Find next palindrome time
    # Start from current time and go forward minute by minute
    current_h, current_m = h, m
    minutes_to_wait = 0
    
    while True:
        minutes_to_wait += 1
        
        # Move to next minute
        current_m += 1
        if current_m >= 60:
            current_m = 0
            current_h += 1
            if current_h >= 24:
                current_h = 0
        
        # Check if new time is palindrome
        new_time = f"{current_h:02d}:{current_m:02d}"
        if is_palindrome(new_time):
            return minutes_to_wait

# Read input
current_time = input().strip()
print(solve(current_time))
```

Wait, I need to be more careful about the palindrome check. Let me reconsider.

For "hh:mm" to be a palindrome when reversed, we need:
- The string "hh:mm" reversed should equal "hh:mm"

For example, "05:50" reversed is "05:50" → this works.
"13:31" reversed is "13:31" → this works.

Actually, let me trace through: "05:39" reversed is "93:50", which is not "05:39".

The condition is: the string reads the same forwards and backwards.

So "hh:mm" is a palindrome if: h1h2:m1m2 = m2m1:h2h1

This means:
- h1 = m2 (first digit of hh equals last digit of mm)
- h2 = m1 (second digit of hh equals first digit of mm)

For "05:50":
- h1 = 0, h2 = 5, m1 = 5, m2 = 0
- h1 = 0 and m2 = 0 ✓
- h2 = 5 and m1 = 5 ✓

For "05:39":
- h1 = 0, h2 = 5, m1 = 3, m2 = 9
- h1 = 0 and m2 = 9 ✗
- h2 = 5 and m1 = 3 ✗

So my palindrome check is correct. Let me verify with the examples:

Example 1: 05:39 → next palindrome should be 05:50 (11 minutes later) ✓
Example 2: 13:31 → already palindrome (0 minutes) ✓  
Example 3: 23:59 → next palindrome should be 00:00 (1 minute later) ✓

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Check if the time string reads the same forwards and backwards
    return time_str == time_str[::-1]

def solve(current_time):
    h, m = map(int, current_time.split(':'))
    
    # Check if current time is already a palindrome
    current_time_str = f"{h:02d}:{m:02d}"
    if is_palindrome(current_time_str):
        return 0
    
    # Find next palindrome time
    current_h, current_m = h, m
    minutes_to_wait = 0
    
    while True:
        minutes_to_wait += 1
        
        # Move to next minute
        current_m += 1
        if current_m >= 60:
            current_m = 0
            current_h += 1
            if current_h >= 24:
                current_h = 0
        
        # Check if new time is palindrome
        new_time_str = f"{current_h:02d}:{current_m:02d}"
        if is_palindrome(new_time_str):
            return minutes_to_wait

# Read input
current_time = input().strip()
print(solve(current_time))
```

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindrome time
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards (e.g., 05:50)
4. We need to consider that after 23:59 comes 00:00, so we might need to wrap around

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Understand palindrome checking
For a time "hh:mm", when reversed, it should read the same. 
- "05:50" reversed becomes "05:50" (same)
- "05:39" reversed becomes "93:50" (different)

### Step 2: How to check if a time is a palindrome
For hh:mm, we can concatenate the digits and check if the string reads the same forwards and backwards.
For "05:50": "0550" → reversed is "0550" → palindrome
For "05:39": "0539" → reversed is "9350" → not palindrome

### Step 3: Algorithm approach
1. Start from the current time and check each subsequent minute
2. For each minute, convert to "hh:mm" format
3. Check if it's a palindrome
4. Return the first palindrome time found (in minutes)

### Step 4: Handle the wrap-around case
Since time goes from 23:59 to 00:00, we need to consider that after 23:59 comes 00:00.

### Step 5: Implementation details
- Convert current time to total minutes since midnight
- For each possible future time (current time + minutes), check if it's a palindrome
- Convert minutes back to hh:mm format
- Check palindrome condition

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Remove colon and check if string reads same forwards and backwards
    digits = time_str.replace(':', '')
    return digits == digits[::-1]

def time_to_minutes(hh, mm):
    return hh * 60 + mm

def minutes_to_time(total_minutes):
    hours = (total_minutes // 60) % 24
    minutes = total_minutes % 60
    return hours, minutes

def solve():
    current_time = input().strip()
    h, m = map(int, current_time.split(':'))
    
    current_minutes = time_to_minutes(h, m)
    
    # Check if current time is already a palindrome
    if is_palindrome(current_time):
        return 0
    
    # Check future times until we find a palindrome
    total_minutes = current_minutes
    while True:
        total_minutes += 1
        if total_minutes >= 24 * 60:
            # We've gone complete cycle, so we need to wrap around to 00:00
            # 00:00 is a palindrome, so we return (24*60 - current_minutes) minutes
            # But since we're adding 1 each time, we need to find the time after wrap
            # Actually, we need to find the next palindrome after the current time
            # After 23:59 comes 00:00, which is a palindrome
            # So if we've gone through a full day, the answer is (24*60 - current_minutes) % (24*60)
            # But we need to be careful about the wrap
            break
        
        hours, minutes = minutes_to_time(total_minutes)
        time_str = f"{hours:02d}:{minutes:02d}"
        if is_palindrome(time_str):
            return total_minutes - current_minutes
    
    # If we've gone through a full day, check if 00:00 is next
    # 00:00 is a palindrome
    hours, minutes = minutes_to_time(0)
    time_str = f"{hours:02d}:{minutes:02d}"
    if is_palindrome(time_str):
        return (24 * 60 - current_minutes) % (24 * 60)

# Simpler approach: just check each minute until we find palindrome
def solve_simple():
    current_time = input().strip()
    h, m = map(int, current_time.split(':'))
    
    current_minutes = h * 60 + m
    
    # Check if current time is already palindrome
    time_str = f"{h:02d}:{m:02d}"
    if is_palindrome(time_str):
        return 0
    
    # Check each subsequent minute
    for minutes_to_add in range(1, 24 * 60):
        new_minutes = current_minutes + minutes_to_add
        hours = (new_minutes // 60) % 24
        minutes = new_minutes % 60
        time_str = f"{hours:02d}:{minutes:02d}"
        if is_palindrome(time_str):
            return minutes_to_add

print(solve_simple())
```
